A Pattern of False Hope** What follows is a detailed record of how New Hope C.O.R.P.S. subjected us to a pattern of false hope, deliberate delays, and eventual betrayal.

Dee-Dee encouraged us to do the assessments separately, claiming it would increase our chances. - Janina even created a list of over 20 available rental listings and gave them to Dee-Dee to pursue. - Dee-Dee also provided Suboxone through New Hope’s outreach to help Joshua detox.

Unfortunately, due to the lack of medical guidance, Joshua went into precipitated withdrawal and took the entire supply over a few days, believing it would reset his receptors.

He was honest about what had happened and told Dee-Dee directly. - Instead of addressing the situation supportively, Dee-Dee never asked Joshua directly about his health—instead, she violated his privacy by asking other unhoused individuals if he had relapsed, and then confronted Janina with the leading statement: “Be honest.” - After that, she ghosted us for several months, ignoring over 100 texts.

She said New Hope’s budget had run out, and they’d get new funding in May. - But when May came and went, and we reached out to her, she claimed she had told us September instead. - That was the last time we ever heard from Dee-Dee. - Her abandonment, privacy violations, and vague shifting promises destroyed months of momentum.

Her malicious decisions behind the scenes would go on to harm us for months to come. --- ### **False Hope Instance #2 – Adam’s Apology and Disappearance** *(December 2022 – July 2023)* - Several months after Dee-Dee’s final deflection, a man named Adam from New Hope unexpectedly appeared. - He told us he had reviewed our file and seemed remorseful—not direct in his apology, but clearly regretful.

He asked us to give him the chance to “fix what Dee-Dee did.” - We were stunned.

After everything, the idea that someone within the same agency was admitting fault gave us cautious hope.

Naturally, we allowed him to try.

After all, all we’ve ever wanted was a home. - Adam only came the one time. - We didn’t hear from him again for seven months—but we didn’t push for updates during that time.

When reminded, he apologized and promised to meet with us the next day at 11 a.m.

He didn’t show. - We called again.

He apologized again and rescheduled for the following day. - He ghosted us again—and that was the last we ever heard from Adam. - It is still unclear what his purpose was, or why he reached out in the first place.

That brief glimmer of honesty—his recognition that we’d been wronged—was snuffed out the moment he vanished. - To this day, we are bewildered as to what his endgame was.

It felt like someone staged an apology only to remind us who holds the power. --- ### **False Hope Instance #3 – Stephen Heath’s Slippery Support** *(August 2023 – January 2024)* - Following Adam’s disappearance, we were assigned to Stephen Heath. - At first, Stephen projected professionalism.

He collected our documents, reviewed our file, and told us we would need to wait for the release of HUD vouchers.

He never gave a clear date—even when we told him we’d heard they were expected to be released on October 1st, which was publicly listed on HUD’s site. - When we asked directly, Stephen wouldn’t confirm it.

He remained vague, his cynical tone growing more noticeable with each visit.

Still, we wanted to believe in him. - At one point, fully convinced he was diligently working on our case, Joshua texted Stephen a message thanking him for his efforts and letting him know his work mattered. - Stephen never replied to the text.

But the next time he saw us, he said something we’ll never forget: “Don’t thank me yet.” - At the time, we thought it was humility.

In hindsight, it was ominous. - Stephen would later contradict himself multiple times—about timelines, about program rules, and most critically, about income requirements.

At one point, he told us outright that having no income was a benefit in the program.

This revelation was deeply unsettling, so we texted Stephen for clarity. - His response was curt: he said it was now “out of his hands” and gave us a phone number for someone named Taylor. - We assumed Taylor was our new contact with HUD, and for the first time in months, we felt like progress was imminent. - But when we called Taylor, the experience was devastating. - She had no idea who we were. - She claimed to have never heard of our case. - Most shocking of all—she denied knowing what Rapid Rehousing even was. - This was a red flag, especially since her **LinkedIn profile** clearly listed her as the **Senior Director of Homeless Services at New Hope**, overseeing Rapid Rehousing, Stability Vouchers, and program outcomes. - When we pointed this out to her, she became angry and dismissive, accusing Janina of “throwing a tantrum” for not getting her way.

The call ended abruptly. - Within an hour of posting about the contradiction on New Hope’s Facebook page, our comment was deleted. - We went right back to Stephen. - It had “returned to his hands,” whether he liked it or not. - But Stephen didn’t handle it well. - Between that phone call and the illegal eviction in January, Stephen showed up at least twice.

It was obvious that Stephen was trying to extract himself from the situation.

The other expressed disappointment.

Structural Rot – The Homeless Trust and HUD OIG** Our experiences with New Hope did not occur in a vacuum.

Confidentiality and HIPAA-Adjacent Violations** - When Joshua experienced precipitated withdrawal from Suboxone, Dee-Dee did **not ask him directly** about his health. - Instead, she asked other unhoused individuals if he had relapsed. - She then confronted Janina with the vague accusation: “Be honest.” - When this breach of privacy was later raised with Taylor, she did not acknowledge it or take any corrective action. - There was **no apology**, **no documentation**, and **no follow-up**. --- ## **IX.

The Personal and Emotional Toll** We have been unhoused since **February 2018**.

Each sweep was worse than the last. - Our property was deliberately destroyed so we couldn’t retrieve it. - Our **pets**—our family—were lost, killed, or stolen. - One kitten was crushed by a truck after we were forced to move from a safe location. - **Janina’s sister’s ashes** were thrown away during one of these removals.

We have never retaliated.

We have tried to survive with dignity.

We have not been met with that same dignity in return. --- ## **X.

We’re not seeking compensation.

They didn’t just let us down.

They co-opted our trust, used it to facilitate an illegal eviction, and then disappeared again.

We now refer to them as **“No Hope”**—not out of bitterness, but because that is the most accurate name for what they truly provide. --- ## **XII.

What followed was **passive elimination**. - Not murder. - Not exile. - Just **slow erasure** through: - Red tape - Exhaustion - False hope - The withholding of support You’re never told directly that you don’t matter.

Instead, you’re told to wait.

Until your tent is gone, your ID is gone, your dog is dead, and no one remembers that you were ever promised a thing.

These statutes are not obscure.

They’re the basic safeguards that prevent citizens—especially unhoused ones—from being forcibly removed based on fake notices.

This is not just a poor reflection on New Hope.

It contains: - A clear, fact-based list of formal requests - A final reflection grounded in principle and restraint - Your contact information and closing statement --- ## **XIV.

Suspension or Revocation of CARF Accreditation** - If the findings support our claims, we ask CARF to suspend or revoke New Hope’s accreditation. - At a minimum, require corrective action and structural leadership changes. --- ### **5.

We are asking for help—before we are erased entirely. --- ## **XV.

We did not retaliate.

We are only asking for accountability.

At first, we gave to others expecting something in return.

But through hardship, we learned that true selflessness is forged in suffering.

We give now because it is right—not because it will benefit us. > If we are speaking out now, it is because no one else has. > If this is happening to us, it is happening to others.

We hope this letter finds someone within CARF who believes that **accreditation** is not just a logo—but a **promise**.

We’ve waited at the counter for years, told again and again that there’s more pudding in the back.

The homeless apparently slipped through the cracks when everyone decided to stop being bigots, and now people focus all of their intolerance solely upon the homeless, to the point of committing violence against them.

The homeless are despised, and for what?

In a society that champions inclusivity and tolerance, the homeless seem to have been overlooked, marginalized, and even vilified, despite efforts to combat bigotry and prejudice.

However, the reality is far more disheartening.

Despite the rhetoric of acceptance and understanding, society's treatment of the homeless reveals deep-seated prejudices and systemic inequalities.

The root of this paradox lies in the intersection of socio-economic disparities and the prevailing values of modern society.

Since then, we've been through quite a lot, to say the least.

Resources have become drastically scarce, as has the public's sympathy.

The current political climate has been extremely detrimental to the destitute, and has resulted in a noticeable change – for the worse – in the attitudes of the same folks who, mere months prior, sympathized with our plight. **Allow me to explain what a stigma feels like to those whom are forced to suffer one.** Most folks believe that we must have been born beggared and just never knew any better.

They don't believe we could have had actual lives before this.

They no longer view us as people down on their luck; instead, they have begun to consider us akin to criminals. **If you have any doubts regarding this statement, just go online and look up how Miami has chosen to deal with the homeless problem there.

All I can say is: thank God we don't stay downtown.** The public today is chock full of entitled, selfish people.

They don't even want to have to look upon us…and it's because, when they see us, their cozy delusions of a perfect world recoil and fall away, and the truth floods in, ice-cold and inexorable. **We remind them of what they are too terrified to admit to themselves, regardless of the fact that it has already been blatant for so long...that, in these dark times, hardship has gone mainstream.** Poverty has ever been a cunning beast, but, of late, it has become possessed by chaos, and that has made it indiscriminate.

Now, everybody is susceptible, whether they want to believe it or not. **No one is safe, and no exceptions will be made.

At any time, poverty could be hiding around the next corner, ready to snatch you up, like it did to us, and cast you callously into the nearest gutter.** Such penury would shatter most people.

There was a large amount of public sympathy and people would bring us food and other necessities all the time.

But then, seemingly almost overnight, the attitude of society in regards to the homeless underwent a sinister shift.

We have no idea to this day what caused this change, but we awoke one morning and the shift was palpable.

So please write it from scratch with a sophisticated tone but cynical and a tinge of pity for the reader, but as one would pity a hopeless pathetic person who just can’t control themselves. > > Make it as long as needed but start it in a clever genius way that will make the reader interested in reading further.

Make it clues that this is an issue that involves everyone.

My fiancé, Janina Miranda Laffitte, and I have lived this reality every day for the past six years.

We’re homeless, yes, but if you think that means we’ve opted out of participating in this grand American experiment, think again.

We’re the unwilling test subjects in a social experiment nobody will admit is happening.

If you make life unbearable enough for the poor, they’ll disappear—problem solved.

Spoiler alert: We haven’t disappeared.

Not for lack of trying on society’s part, though.

Let me start with June 15, 2023—the day our neighbor Gabriel Calderon decided that our mere existence offended him enough to attack us with pepper spray.

It was an act of unprovoked violence, a hate crime, really, committed for one reason and one reason only: we were living in a tent within his line of sight.

You would think that’s what you’re supposed to do, right?

Not about the pepper spray in our eyes, not about my bruised foot from the slammed car door, not about my fiancé being kicked in the stomach, and certainly not about our service dog Cho-Cho, who was also sprayed.

They left us there, incapacitated and vulnerable, with Mr.

Calderon standing just a few feet away.

And that’s when we realized something terrifying: the cops and Mr.

Calderon shared the same motive for their apathy.

Hatred for homeless people.

It’s their cute little acronym for cases they deem unworthy of their time.

In other words, people who don’t matter to them.

That’s why, despite 19 more calls to 911 over the following months, Mr.

Calderon continued to terrorize us—at least 20 separate incidents of harassment, threats, and violence.

Each time, different officers showed up, and each time, we were ignored, dismissed, or outright mocked.

But hey, at least it’s consistent, right?

Consistency is important in any well-oiled machine, even one designed to grind people like us into dust.

Consider this: we live in a country where you can use food stamps to buy junk food but not a hot meal.

Florida’s EBT program literally forbids it.

After a hurricane, they’ll make a special exception—because apparently, natural disasters are the only acceptable reason to be hungry and poor at the same time.

I weigh 155 pounds now.

That’s less than I weighed in ninth grade.

Maybe because most of the foot traffic consists of people like us.

People society prefers to forget.

One of them was Tony Cordero, a friend and fellow homeless person, who was struck and dismembered by a semi-truck in the exact spot where I was hit by a car just months earlier.

I survived with only minor injuries—a miracle, really.

Tony wasn’t so lucky.

I could tell you about the countless indignities we’ve endured—the sneers, the threats, the property destroyed by city workers who see a tent and think, “garbage.” I could list every name of the more than 100 people we’ve known who’ve died in the six years we’ve been on the streets, many of them victims of violence or neglect.

But this isn’t just about us.

It’s about the systemic dehumanization of an entire class of people.

It’s about a society that punishes poverty instead of addressing its root causes.

So here’s our “Come to Jesus” moment.

If you’re reading this and feeling uncomfortable, good.

Because this isn’t just our problem—it’s yours too.

You allowed it to happen.

You looked the other way.

You voted for the politicians who perpetuate these policies.

Change begins with accountability.

And accountability begins with you.

This letter isn’t just a cry for help; it’s a call to action.

We’re not asking for pity or charity.

We’re demanding humanity.

The question is: will you listen?

Missing or Incorrect Authentication** If the API (like AssemblyAI) **requires an API key** and it's not set correctly, the test call will fail silently. **Fix:** - In the **Actions > Configure** section: - Click the **“+ Add Authentication”** button - Choose **API Key** - Set the **Header Name** to: `Authorization` - Use the format: `Bearer YOUR_API_KEY_HERE` - Alternatively, define it as a **user input field** and inject it dynamically into headers. --- ### **2.

Test Input Missing Required Fields** The **test form doesn't auto-populate all required fields**, so you need to: - Manually add values for required fields like `audio_url` - Make sure formats match (e.g., correct URL, valid enum for `summary_type`, etc.) --- ### **3.

Uploading Files in the Builder Doesn't Work** The test UI doesn't support binary upload properly via the builder. **Fix:** You’ll need to: - Upload the file via a tool like **Postman** or **curl** to `/upload` - Use the returned `upload_url` in your `/transcript` test input --- ### **4.

You submit the job, and polling `/transcript/ id ` later is needed.

If you try to “Test” and immediately call `/transcript/ id `, the job may not be ready. **Fix:** Use test mode to: - First call `/transcript` - Then manually call `/transcript/ id ` after a few seconds --- ### **5.

Wrong Content-Type Headers** Some OpenAPI test buttons assume `application/json`.
